    "originalFile": "\\begin{abstract}\nWe present \\textsc{OntoCoDec}, a framework that translates Information Extraction (IE) ontologies into composable token-level decoding constraints.\nA decidability analysis yields a two-layer architecture: a \\emph{grammar layer} (context-free) compiles ontology constraints into grammars ensuring JSON validity and vocabulary membership; a \\emph{semantic layer} (prefix-decidable with state) interprets type constraints at runtime via stateful token masking over a dynamic entity registry, with grammar-first fallback when constraints conflict.\nThe two layers exhibit an empirical enabling dependency (Proposition~1, scale-sensitive; \\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}): neither achieves full DR reduction alone.\n% exp_ids: w5_lora_scierc_constrained_eval, w5_qwen_docred_lora_constrained_eval, w5_llama_lora_scierc_constrained_eval, w5_llama_docred_lora_constrained_eval\nComposed with LoRA fine-tuning on three 8B-class models across two RE datasets (up to 96 relation types), \\textsc{OntoCoDec} near-eliminates DR ($<$\\,0.1\\%) at R-F1 41--46\\% ($\\pm$0.8\\,pp); under fine-tuning, CF remains $\\leq$\\,1.4\\% with greedy decoding and reaches zero with beam search ($k{=}4$) on both RE datasets (1.54$\\times$ latency; \\S\\ref{sec:beam-results}).\nZero-shot evaluation serves as a diagnostic probe, revealing DR reduction of 76--99\\% on SciERC and 42--90\\% on DocRED across three models; constraint tightness ($\\bar{\\kappa}$) is a descriptive correlate of enforcement difficulty ($n{=}3$ schemas, $p{=}0.138$).\nPreliminary 70B-class validation (\\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}) and event extraction on WikiEvents (\\S\\ref{sec:wikievents}) confirm generalizability beyond the primary 8B evaluation.\n\\end{abstract}\n",

```text
18	A decidability analysis of IE type constraints (\S\ref{sec:decidability}) yields a two-layer architecture: the \emph{grammar layer} compiles ontology constraints into context-free grammars (JSON structure, vocabulary membership) via XGrammar; the \emph{semantic layer} interprets type constraints at runtime via dynamic logit masking over an entity registry, with an \emph{empirical enabling dependency} between layers.
19	OntoCoDec compiles constraints automatically from IE ontologies, requires no constraint-specific training, and is applicable to autoregressive LMs with logits access; we validate on three English datasets (up to 96 relation types), three 8B-class models across three tokenizer families, with preliminary 70B-class validation (\S\ref{sec:70b-results}).
20	LoRA + greedy S1 achieves near-complete DR elimination: DR\,$\leq$\,0.09\% at R-F1 41--46\% ($\pm$0.8\,pp, $+$10\% latency); beam ($k{=}4$) eliminates all violation types on both RE datasets under fine-tuning (1.54$\times$ latency).
21	DR enforcement converts ontologically invalid violations to individually valid but type-inconsistent ones (CF\,$\leq$\,1.4\% fine-tuned; \S\ref{sec:scierc-results}); zero-shot evaluation reveals DR drops 42--99\% across models and datasets, with 76--99\% on SciERC, but total violations (DR+CF) decrease by $\sim$31\%, reflecting conversion that fine-tuning resolves (\S\ref{sec:cross-model}).
22	
23	We make a systematic engineering contribution with theoretical grounding:
24	\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt]
25	    \item A two-layer constrained decoding framework: we formalize a well-known intuition that cross-field type constraints require runtime state beyond context-free expressiveness (Proposition~1), yielding pair-based sequential enforcement at each level (\S\ref{sec:decidability}). Constraints compile automatically from IE ontologies, validated on schemas of varying restrictiveness with up to 96 relation types.
26	    \item A comprehensive evaluation across 3 IE datasets, 3 models (8B-class), and 2 tasks, with preliminary 70B validation: under fine-tuning, DR\,$\leq$\,0.09\% at R-F1 41--46\% with CF\,$\leq$\,1.4\%; zero-shot ablation reveals an enabling dependency where on SciERC neither layer alone exceeds 24\% DR reduction (at 70B, semantic layer achieves 84\% independently; \S\ref{sec:70b-results}); event extraction on WikiEvents validates applicability beyond RE (\S\ref{sec:wikievents}).
27	\end{enumerate}
28	
```

    "originalFile": "% Section 1: Introduction (compressed, 669→~550 words)\n\n\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nLarge language models are increasingly used for information extraction~\\cite{li2023codeie,sainz2024gollie,wei2023zeroshot}, but when generating relation triples $(h, r, t)$, neither entity necessarily carries a type consistent with the ontology's domain/range constraints for $r$.\nWe call such outputs \\emph{domain/range violations}: structurally valid triples that are ontologically invalid.\nViolations persist across the capability spectrum: 15.4\\% of triples on SciERC~\\cite{luan2018scierc} and 18.5\\% on DocRED~\\cite{yao2019docred} under zero-shot decoding, and 0.5--2\\% even after LoRA fine-tuning; rates correlate with schema restrictiveness (few valid type pairs per relation) rather than ontology or model scale.\nAt competitive quality (R-F1 40--46\\%), even 1--2\\% residual violations compound across thousands of documents into systematic knowledge graph corruption, making reliable constraint enforcement necessary.\n\nExisting approaches leave a fundamental gap.\nSchema-informed prompting reduces violations by 62\\% on average but is per-relation unreliable (violations for \\textsc{Evaluate-for} actually \\emph{increase} by 5.8\\,pp).\nGrammar-constrained decoding~\\cite{dong2025xgrammar,willard2023outlines,beurer-kellner2023lmql} ensures syntactic well-formedness but cannot encode cross-field semantic constraints.\nPost-hoc filtering improves precision but discards up to 48\\% of predictions including true positives (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-model}); at high rejection rates (e.g., Mistral DocRED 73.7\\%), it becomes impractical.\nTraining-based approaches~\\cite{sainz2024gollie,lu2022uie} internalize schema knowledge but provide only soft guidance without runtime enforcement; no method provides both semantic awareness and hard constraint enforcement.\n\nWe propose \\textbf{OntoCoDec}, a framework that translates IE ontologies into composable layers of decoding-time constraints.\nA decidability analysis of IE type constraints (\\S\\ref{sec:decidability}) yields a two-layer architecture: the \\emph{grammar layer} compiles ontology constraints into context-free grammars (JSON structure, vocabulary membership) via XGrammar; the \\emph{semantic layer} interprets type constraints at runtime via dynamic logit masking over an entity registry, with an \\emph{empirical enabling dependency} between layers.\nOntoCoDec compiles constraints automatically from IE ontologies, requires no constraint-specific training, and is applicable to autoregressive LMs with logits access; 2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - OntoCoDec: Two-layer constrained decoding (grammar CFG + semantic context-sensitive) for IE
   - Enabling dependency: scale-sensitive — 70B semantic-only achieves 84% DR reduction vs 8B's 24%
   - Two deployment tiers: greedy S1 (+10% latency, DR<0.1%, CF≤1.4%) vs beam S1 (1.54× latency, all violations→0%)
   - Violation types: DR (domain/range), CF (cross-field), RV (relation vocabulary), ET (entity type)
   - S2 (two-pass) scale-dependent degradation: at 70B, R-F1 drops 8.45pp while 8B shows comparable S1/S2
   - Order invariance: shuffling schema enumeration produces byte-identical outputs
   - EMNLP 2026: 8-page main text limit, unlimited Limitations/references/appendix
   - Registry as truth source for all quantitative claims
   - R-F1 range across all fine-tuned configurations: 40.79–46.97 → reported as "41–47%"

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **abstract.tex** — Core paper claims
     - Changed "41--46" → "41--47" (R-F1 range)
     - Changed "residual cross-field violations (CF≤1.4%) represent violation conversion; beam eliminates all four violation types" → "under fine-tuning, CF remains ≤1.4% with greedy decoding and reaches zero with beam search (k=4) on both RE datasets"
     - Current key line: `Composed with LoRA fine-tuning on three 8B-class models across two RE datasets (up to 96 relation types), \textsc{OntoCoDec} near-eliminates DR ($<$\,0.1\%) at R-F1 41--47\% ($\pm$0.8\,pp); under fine-tuning, CF remains $\leq$\,1.4\% with greedy decoding and reaches zero with beam search ($k{=}4$) on both RE datasets (1.54$\times$ latency; \S\ref{sec:beam-results}).`

     - Added high-κ reference: "high-$\bar{\kappa}$ relations account for only 3.8\% of gold triples in DocRED (Appendix~\ref{app:kappa-analysis})"

Grammar = GCD ceiling~\cite{geng2023gcd}. $\dagger$vLLM \texttt{guided\_json}~\cite{kwon2023vllm} (\S\ref{sec:constrained-baselines}).}`
     - Merged S2 + appendix pointer into one sentence

   - **section5_docred_draft.tex** — DocRED results

     - Fixed `\ref{app:power-analysis}` → `\ref{app:power-analysis-full}` (line 66)

   - **section6_related_work.tex** — Related work
     - Compressed PICARD sentence: removed "PICARD prunes invalid beam hypotheses post-generation, whereas" and "rather than single-output SQL queries"

   - **section7_conclusion_compressed.tex** — Discussion and Conclusion (CRITICAL for page budget)
     - Expanded sentence 1: added "providing a systematic empirical framework---automatically compiled from ontologies, validated across 3 datasets, 3+ models, and 2 tasks---rather than a novel decoding algorithm" (R16-W5 novelty positioning)
     - Modified central insight: "unlike rejection-based approaches that prune invalid outputs post-generation~\cite{scholak2021picard}, the semantic layer applies constructive forward masking via a dynamic entity registry" (R16-W5 PICARD distinction)
     - Modified last sentence: "extending to global entity-level type consensus during decoding---eliminating CF without beam search overhead---is the most important direction for future work" (R16-MF3 future work)
     - Full current content:
       ```
       OntoCoDec translates IE ontologies into composable token-level constraints, providing a systematic empirical framework---automatically compiled from ontologies, validated across 3 datasets, 3+ models, and 2 tasks---rather than a novel decoding algorithm.
       LoRA + greedy S1 near-eliminates DR ($<$\,0.1\%) with CF\,$\leq$\,1.4\%; beam ($k{=}4$) eliminates all violations on both RE datasets (1.54$\times$ latency); zero-shot DR drops 42--99\% across three 8B-class models.
       The central insight is that IE type constraints exceed context-free expressiveness (Proposition~1): unlike rejection-based approaches that prune invalid outputs post-generation~\cite{scholak2021picard}, the semantic layer applies constructive forward masking via a dynamic entity registry, bridging the gap grammar-only enforcement cannot address (3\% vs.\ 94\% DR reduction); this enabling dependency---scale-sensitive (\S\ref{sec:70b-results})---shows grammar-constrained decoding is necessary but insufficient for IE schema compliance.
       Residual CF arises from per-triple independent enforcement; extending to global entity-level type consensus during decoding---eliminating CF without beam search overhead---is the most important direction for future work.
       ```

     - 5-sentence analysis paragraph covering: grammar zero-contribution, semantic-only as sole defense, S2 scale-dependent degradation, beam4 optimal, CF reversal observation
     - Current analysis text includes: "Notably, S2 increases CF from 15 (S1) to 18 despite its cross-field consistency design, suggesting that over-constraining induces cascading generation degradation that creates new violations."

     - Added `\input{appendix_order_invariance}` between appendix_high_kappa_analysis and appendix_pumping_lemma

        "content": "% Section 1: Introduction (compressed, 669→~550 words)\n\n\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nLarge language models are increasingly used for information extraction~\\cite{li2023codeie,sainz2024gollie,wei2023zeroshot}, but when generating relation triples $(h, r, t)$, neither entity necessarily carries a type consistent with the ontology's domain/range constraints for $r$.\nWe call such outputs \\emph{domain/range violations}: structurally valid triples that are ontologically invalid.\nViolations persist across the capability spectrum: 15.4\\% of triples on SciERC~\\cite{luan2018scierc} and 18.5\\% on DocRED~\\cite{yao2019docred} under zero-shot decoding, and 0.5--2\\% even after LoRA fine-tuning; rates correlate with schema restrictiveness (few valid type pairs per relation) rather than ontology or model scale.\nAt competitive quality (R-F1 40--46\\%), even 1--2\\% residual violations compound across thousands of documents into systematic knowledge graph corruption, making reliable constraint enforcement necessary.\n\nExisting approaches leave a fundamental gap.\nSchema-informed prompting reduces violations by 62\\% on average but is per-relation unreliable (violations for \\textsc{Evaluate-for} actually \\emph{increase} by 5.8\\,pp).\nGrammar-constrained decoding~\\cite{dong2025xgrammar,willard2023outlines,beurer-kellner2023lmql} ensures syntactic well-formedness but cannot encode cross-field semantic constraints.\nPost-hoc filtering improves precision but discards up to 48\\% of predictions including true positives (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-model}); at high rejection rates (e.g., Mistral DocRED 73.7\\%), it becomes impractical.\nTraining-based approaches~\\cite{sainz2024gollie,lu2022uie} internalize schema knowledge but provide only soft guidance without runtime enforcement; no method provides both semantic awareness and hard constraint enforcement.\n\nWe propose \\textbf{OntoCoDec}, a framework that translates IE ontologies into composable layers of decoding-time constraints.\nA decidability analysis of IE type constraints (\\S\\ref{sec:decidability}) yields a two-layer architecture: the \\emph{grammar layer} compiles ontology constraints into context-free grammars (JSON structure, vocabulary membership) via XGrammar; the \\emph{semantic layer} interprets type constraints at runtime via dynamic logit masking over an entity registry, with an \\emph{empirical enabling dependency} between layers.\nOntoCoDec compiles constraints automatically from IE ontologies, requires no constraint-specific training, and is applicable to autoregressive LMs with logits access; we validate on three English datasets (up to 96 relation types), three 8B-class models across three tokenizer families, with preliminary 70B-class validation (\\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}).\nLoRA + greedy S1 achieves near-complete DR elimination: DR\\,$\\leq$\\,0.09\\% at R-F1 41--47\\% ($\\pm$0.8\\,pp, $+$10\\% latency); beam ($k{=}4$) eliminates all violation types on both RE datasets under fine-tuning (1.54$\\times$ latency).\nDR enforcement converts ontologically invalid violations to individually valid but type-inconsistent ones (CF\\,$\\leq$\\,1.4\\% fine-tuned; \\S\\ref{sec:scierc-results}); zero-shot evaluation reveals DR drops 42--99\\% across models and datasets, with 76--99\\% on SciERC, but total violations (DR+CF) decrease by $\\sim$31\\%, reflecting conversion that fine-tuning resolves (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-model}).\n\nWe make a systematic engineering contribution with theoretical grounding:\n\\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt]\n    \\item A two-layer constrained decoding framework: we formalize a well-known intuition that cross-field type constraints require runtime state beyond context-free expressiveness (Proposition~1), yielding pair-based sequential enforcement at each level (\\S\\ref{sec:decidability}). Constraints compile automatically from IE ontologies, validated on schemas of varying restrictiveness with up to 96 relation types.\n    \\item A comprehensive evaluation across 3 IE datasets, 3 models (8B-class), and 2 tasks, with preliminary 70B validation: under fine-tuning, DR\\,$\\leq$\\,0.09\\% at R-F1 41--47\\% with CF\\,$\\leq$\\,1.4\\%; zero-shot ablation reveals an enabling dependency where on SciERC neither layer alone exceeds 24\\% DR reduction (at 70B, semantic layer achieves 84\\% independently; \\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}); event extraction on WikiEvents validates applicability beyond RE (\\S\\ref{sec:wikievents}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",
        "numLines": 28,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 28
      }
    },

  },

    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_6/projects/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/artifacts/_project/paper/abstract.tex",
        "content": "\\begin{abstract}\nWe present \\textsc{OntoCoDec}, a framework that translates Information Extraction (IE) ontologies into composable token-level decoding constraints.\nA decidability analysis yields a two-layer architecture: a \\emph{grammar layer} (context-free) compiles ontology constraints into grammars ensuring JSON validity and vocabulary membership; a \\emph{semantic layer} (prefix-decidable with state) interprets type constraints at runtime via stateful token masking over a dynamic entity registry, with grammar-first fallback when constraints conflict.\nThe two layers exhibit an empirical enabling dependency (Proposition~1, scale-sensitive; \\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}): neither achieves full DR reduction alone.\n% exp_ids: w5_lora_scierc_constrained_eval, w5_qwen_docred_lora_constrained_eval, w5_llama_lora_scierc_constrained_eval, w5_llama_docred_lora_constrained_eval\nComposed with LoRA fine-tuning on three 8B-class models across two RE datasets (up to 96 relation types), \\textsc{OntoCoDec} near-eliminates DR ($<$\\,0.1\\%) at R-F1 41--47\\% ($\\pm$0.8\\,pp); under fine-tuning, CF remains $\\leq$\\,1.4\\% with greedy decoding and reaches zero with beam search ($k{=}4$) on both RE datasets (1.54$\\times$ latency; \\S\\ref{sec:beam-results}).\nZero-shot evaluation serves as a diagnostic probe, revealing DR reduction of 76--99\\% on SciERC and 42--90\\% on DocRED across three models; constraint tightness ($\\bar{\\kappa}$) is a descriptive correlate of enforcement difficulty ($n{=}3$ schemas, $p{=}0.138$).\nPreliminary 70B-class validation (\\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}) and event extraction on WikiEvents (\\S\\ref{sec:wikievents}) confirm generalizability beyond the primary 8B evaluation.\n\\end{abstract}\n",
        "numLines": 10,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 10
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../artifacts/_project/paper/abstract.tex"
  },

{
  "parentUuid": "5a2eb351-0a22-4a16-a58d-869ff0854fcc",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_6/projects/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/artifacts/_project/paper/appendix_70b_ablation.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_6/projects/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/artifacts/_project/paper/appendix_70b_ablation.tex",
        "content": "% Appendix: 70B-Class LoRA Ablation on SciERC\n% exp_ids: eval_70b_lora_scierc_unconstrained, eval_70b_lora_scierc_grammar_only,\n%          eval_70b_lora_scierc_semantic_only, eval_70b_lora_scierc_s1_constrained,\n%          eval_70b_lora_scierc_s2_constrained, eval_70b_lora_scierc_beam4_s1\n\n\\paragraph{70B-class fine-tuned ablation.}\n\\label{app:70b-ablation}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:70b-ft-ablation} presents the constraint ablation on Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct (AWQ INT4) with LoRA fine-tuning on SciERC (551 documents).\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3.6pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}l rrrr rr@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Mode}\n  & \\textbf{DR\\,$\\downarrow$} & \\textbf{RV\\,$\\downarrow$} & \\textbf{ET\\,$\\downarrow$} & \\textbf{CF}\n  & \\textbf{R-F1\\,$\\uparrow$} & \\textbf{E-F1\\,$\\uparrow$} \\\\\n\\midrule\nUnconstrained & 1.50 & 0 & 0 & 0    & 44.73 & 64.86 \\\\\nGrammar       & 1.50 & 0 & 0 & 0    & 44.63 & 64.82 \\\\\nSemantic      & 0.60 & 0 & 0 & 9    & 44.73 & 64.86 \\\\\nS1            & \\textbf{0} & 0 & 0 & 15 & 44.53 & 64.76 \\\\\nS2            & \\textbf{0} & 0 & 0 & 18 & 36.28 & 62.35 \\\\\nBeam4 S1      & \\textbf{0} & 0 & 0 & \\textbf{1} & \\textbf{44.81} & \\textbf{64.89} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{70B-class (Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct (AWQ INT4)) LoRA ablation on SciERC (551 docs).\nCF: violation count. F1 $\\times$100.}\n\\label{tab:70b-ft-ablation}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe grammar layer provides zero DR reduction (1.50\\%\\,$\\to$\\,1.50\\%), consistent with the zero-shot 70B pattern where fine-tuned models already produce structurally valid JSON (\\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}).\nThe semantic layer is the sole effective defense (1.50\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0.60\\%, 60\\% reduction) with no R-F1 cost ($\\Delta{=}$0\\,pp), and full S1 eliminates all DR.\nS2 eliminates DR but causes severe R-F1 degradation ($-$8.45\\,pp), indicating that optimal constraint granularity is scale-dependent: the additional S2 constraints become overly restrictive at 70B, where stronger baseline generation makes fine-grained intervention counterproductive.\nBeam ($k{=}4$) S1 remains the optimal configuration (DR${=}$0\\%, CF${=}$1, R-F1${=}$44.81).\nNotably, S2 increases CF from 15 (S1) to 18 despite its cross-field consistency design, suggesting that over-constraining induces cascading generation degradation that creates new violations.\n",
        "numLines": 39,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 39
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../artifacts/_project/paper/appendix_70b_ablation.tex"

        "content": "% Runtime overhead (inline paragraph, no subsection header to save space)\n\n\\paragraph{Runtime overhead.}\n\\label{sec:runtime}\nS1 overhead is $+$13.1\\% on SciERC, lower on DocRED due to amortization over longer sequences; under LoRA, $+$10.0\\% with 37\\% lower absolute latency.\nConstraint compilation is one-time $<$0.5\\,s; scaling benchmarks confirm constant overhead (2--3\\%) up to 200 entity types and 1{,}000 relations (Appendix~\\ref{app:scalability}).\n\n\\paragraph{Beam search compatibility.}\n\\label{sec:beam-results}\nWith LoRA, beam search ($k{=}4$)~\\cite{anderson2017guided} eliminates all four violation types on both SciERC and DocRED (R-F1 43--47; Appendix~\\ref{app:beam-search}), yielding two deployment tiers: greedy S1 ($+$10\\% latency, DR${<}$0.1\\%, CF${\\leq}$1.4\\%) and beam S1 (1.54$\\times$ latency, all violations${\\to}$0\\%; Table~\\ref{tab:beam-latency}).\n\n% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_s1, r16_70b_docred_zs_s1, 70b_lora_scierc, 70b_lora_docred_200\n\\paragraph{Preliminary 70B-class validation.}\n\\label{sec:70b-results}\nOn Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct (AWQ INT4), zero-shot S1 eliminates DR on both datasets: SciERC 6.41\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\% (R-F1 11.24), DocRED 14.98\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\% (R-F1 13.05); CF rises to 0.04--3.50\\%, comparable to 8B zero-shot CF.\n% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_ablation (grammar_only, semantic_only, full_s1)\nA four-way ablation on SciERC reveals scale-dependent layer interaction: semantic-only achieves 84\\% DR reduction (6.41\\%\\,$\\to$\\,1.01\\%), far exceeding the 8B result (24\\%; Table~\\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}), reflecting stronger internalized JSON generation; grammar remains necessary for RV elimination (0.61\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\%) and CF control (semantic-only CF${=}$76).\n% exp_ids: eval_70b_lora_scierc_s1_constrained, eval_70b_lora_docred_200_s1_constrained\nLoRA confirms composability: DR\\,$\\to$\\,0\\% on both datasets (R-F1 44.53, 43.95; $|\\Delta| \\leq 0.20$\\,pp); CF drops to 0.18--1.33\\%, consistent with the 8B pattern (\\S\\ref{sec:composability}).\n\n% exp_ids: trie_qwen_ft_scierc, trie_qwen_zs_scierc, trie_llama_ft_scierc, trie_llama_zs_scierc\n\\paragraph{Comparison with grammar-constrained approaches.}\n\\label{sec:constrained-baselines}\nOur grammar-only ablation (Table~\\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}, row~2) implements the same constraints as GCD~\\cite{geng2023gcd} and BoostCD~\\cite{boostcd2025}; vLLM \\texttt{guided\\_json}~\\cite{kwon2023vllm} confirms equivalence (DR${=}$15.26\\%, R-F1${=}$8.75; GCD-equiv.\\ row, Appendix~\\ref{app:gcd-comparison}).\nThe ${\\sim}$15\\% residual DR is the gap our semantic layer addresses (94\\% total reduction).\nTrie-only constraints without JSON enforcement yield 9--51\\% parse failure (Appendix~\\ref{app:trie-baseline}).\n",

      "- Explore: Fast read-only search agent for locating code. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. <details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_014eQQiMEjyx6vW1fbT7PHqq`</summary>

```text
1	% Runtime overhead (inline paragraph, no subsection header to save space)
2	
3	\paragraph{Runtime overhead.}
4	\label{sec:runtime}
5	S1 overhead is $+$13.1\% on SciERC, lower on DocRED due to amortization over longer sequences; under LoRA, $+$10.0\% with 37\% lower absolute latency.
6	Constraint compilation is one-time $<$0.5\,s; scaling benchmarks confirm constant overhead (2--3\%) up to 200 entity types and 1{,}000 relations (Appendix~\ref{app:scalability}).
7	
8	\paragraph{Beam search compatibility.}
9	\label{sec:beam-results}
10	With LoRA, beam search ($k{=}4$)~\cite{anderson2017guided} eliminates all four violation types on both SciERC and DocRED (R-F1 43--47; Appendix~\ref{app:beam-search}), yielding two deployment tiers: greedy S1 ($+$10\% latency, DR${<}$0.1\%, CF${\leq}$1.4\%) and beam S1 (1.54$\times$ latency, all violations${\to}$0\%; Table~\ref{tab:beam-latency}).
11	
12	% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_s1, r16_70b_docred_zs_s1, 70b_lora_scierc, 70b_lora_docred_200
13	\paragraph{Preliminary 70B-class validation.}
14	\label{sec:70b-results}
15	On Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct (AWQ INT4), zero-shot S1 eliminates DR on both datasets: SciERC 6.41\%\,$\to$\,0\% (R-F1 11.24), DocRED 14.98\%\,$\to$\,0\% (R-F1 13.05); CF rises to 0.04--3.50\%, comparable to 8B zero-shot CF.
16	% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_ablation (grammar_only, semantic_only, full_s1)
17	A four-way ablation on SciERC reveals scale-dependent layer interaction: semantic-only achieves 84\% DR reduction (6.41\%\,$\to$\,1.01\%), far exceeding the 8B result (24\%; Table~\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}), reflecting stronger internalized JSON generation; grammar remains necessary for RV elimination (0.61\%\,$\to$\,0\%) and CF control (semantic-only CF${=}$76).
18	% exp_ids: eval_70b_lora_scierc_s1_constrained, eval_70b_lora_docred_200_s1_constrained
19	LoRA confirms composability: DR\,$\to$\,0\% on both datasets (R-F1 44.53, 43.95; $|\Delta| \leq 0.20$\,pp); CF drops to 0.18--1.33\%, consistent with the 8B pattern (\S\ref{sec:composability}).
20	
21	% exp_ids: trie_qwen_ft_scierc, trie_qwen_zs_scierc, trie_llama_ft_scierc, trie_llama_zs_scierc
22	\paragraph{Comparison with grammar-constrained approaches.}
23	\label{sec:constrained-baselines}
24	Our grammar-only ablation (Table~\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}, row~2) implements the same constraints as GCD~\cite{geng2023gcd} and BoostCD~\cite{boostcd2025}; vLLM \texttt{guided\_json}~\cite{kwon2023vllm} confirms equivalence (DR${=}$15.26\%, R-F1${=}$8.75; GCD-equiv.\ row, Appendix~\ref{app:gcd-comparison}).
25	The ${\sim}$15\% residual DR is the gap our semantic layer addresses (94\% total reduction).
26	Trie-only constraints without JSON enforcement yield 9--51\% parse failure (Appendix~\ref{app:trie-baseline}).
27	
```

6	\paragraph{70B-class fine-tuned ablation.}
7	\label{app:70b-ablation}
8	
9	Table~\ref{tab:70b-ft-ablation} presents the constraint ablation on Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct (AWQ INT4) with LoRA fine-tuning on SciERC (551 documents).
10	
11	\begin{table}[h]
12	\centering
13	\small
14	\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3.6pt}
15	\begin{tabular}{@{}l rrrr rr@{}}
16	\toprule
17	\textbf{Mode}
18	  & \textbf{DR\,$\downarrow$} & \textbf{RV\,$\downarrow$} & \textbf{ET\,$\downarrow$} & \textbf{CF}
19	  & \textbf{R-F1\,$\uparrow$} & \textbf{E-F1\,$\uparrow$} \\
20	\midrule
21	Unconstrained & 1.50 & 0 & 0 & 0    & 44.73 & 64.86 \\
22	Grammar       & 1.50 & 0 & 0 & 0    & 44.63 & 64.82 \\
23	Semantic      & 0.60 & 0 & 0 & 9    & 44.73 & 64.86 \\
24	S1            & \textbf{0} & 0 & 0 & 15 & 44.53 & 64.76 \\
25	S2            & \textbf{0} & 0 & 0 & 18 & 36.28 & 62.35 \\
26	Beam4 S1      & \textbf{0} & 0 & 0 & \textbf{1} & \textbf{44.81} & \textbf{64.89} \\
27	\bottomrule
28	\end{tabular}
29	\caption{70B-class (Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct (AWQ INT4)) LoRA ablation on SciERC (551 docs).
30	CF: violation count. F1 $\times$100.}
31	\label{tab:70b-ft-ablation}
32	\end{table}
33	
34	The grammar layer provides zero DR reduction (1.50\%\,$\to$\,1.50\%), consistent with the zero-shot 70B pattern where fine-tuned models already produce structurally valid JSON (\S\ref{sec:70b-results}).
35	The semantic layer is the sole effective defense (1.50\%\,$\to$\,0.60\%, 60\% reduction) with no R-F1 cost ($\Delta{=}$0\,pp), and full S1 eliminates all DR.
36	S2 eliminates DR but causes severe R-F1 degradation ($-$8.45\,pp), indicating that optimal constraint granularity is scale-dependent: the additional S2 constraints become overly restrictive at 70B, where stronger baseline generation makes fine-grained intervention counterproductive.
37	Beam ($k{=}4$) S1 remains the optimal configuration (DR${=}$0\%, CF${=}$1, R-F1${=}$44.81).
38	Notably, S2 increases CF from 15 (S1) to 18 despite its cross-field consistency design, suggesting that over-constraining induces cascading generation degradation that creates new violations.
39

[Director 指令] [Patch 修正] Reviewer 指出 DocRED beam4 +4.19pp 被 parse failure recovery confound，修正之前的写作指令：

**关键 caveat：** DocRED greedy parse failure=12.5%（25/200 docs），beam=0.5%（1/200）。恢复的 ~24 docs 是 R-F1 +4.19pp 的主要来源。SciERC parse failure 在两种设置下都是 0%，beam boost 仅 +0.28pp——这是"纯"beam 质量提升。

**§5.5 70B 段落修正（替代之前的指令）：**
写 DocRED beam4 时，措辞必须包含 parse failure caveat。建议：
"On DocRED, beam search additionally reduces parse failures from 12.5\% to 0.5\%, contributing to a +4.19pp R-F1 improvement (43.95$\to$48.14); the controlled comparison on SciERC (0\% parse failure in both settings) isolates the pure beam quality gain at +0.28pp."

**不要：**
- 不要 frame 为 "beam improves quality"
- 不要把 48.14 放入 Abstract/Intro
- 不要改 R-F1 range "41-47%"

**appendix 70B 消融表：** DocRED section 照常加入 beam4 + greedy S1 + unconstrained 三行数据，但在脚注标注 parse failure rate 差异。

编译确认后回复。

1	% Runtime overhead (inline paragraph, no subsection header to save space)

3	\paragraph{Runtime overhead.}
4	\label{sec:runtime}
5	S1 overhead is $+$13.1\% on SciERC, lower on DocRED due to amortization over longer sequences; under LoRA, $+$10.0\% with 37\% lower absolute latency.
6	Constraint compilation is one-time $<$0.5\,s; scaling benchmarks confirm constant overhead (2--3\%) up to 200 entity types and 1{,}000 relations (Appendix~\ref{app:scalability}).

8	\paragraph{Beam search compatibility.}
9	\label{sec:beam-results}
10	With LoRA, beam search ($k{=}4$)~\cite{anderson2017guided} eliminates all four violation types on both SciERC and DocRED (R-F1 43--47; Appendix~\ref{app:beam-search}), yielding two deployment tiers: greedy S1 ($+$10\% latency, DR${<}$0.1\%, CF${\leq}$1.4\%) and beam S1 (1.54$\times$ latency, all violations${\to}$0\%; Table~\ref{tab:beam-latency}).
11	
12	% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_s1, r16_70b_docred_zs_s1, 70b_lora_scierc, 70b_lora_docred_200
13	\paragraph{Preliminary 70B-class validation.}
14	\label{sec:70b-results}
15	On Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct (AWQ INT4), zero-shot S1 eliminates DR on both datasets: SciERC 6.41\%\,$\to$\,0\% (R-F1 11.24), DocRED 14.98\%\,$\to$\,0\% (R-F1 13.05); CF rises to 0.04--3.50\%, comparable to 8B zero-shot CF.
16	% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_ablation (grammar_only, semantic_only, full_s1)
17	A four-way ablation on SciERC reveals scale-dependent layer interaction: semantic-only achieves 84\% DR reduction (6.41\%\,$\to$\,1.01\%), far exceeding the 8B result (24\%; Table~\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}), reflecting stronger internalized JSON generation; grammar remains necessary for RV elimination (0.61\%\,$\to$\,0\%) and CF control (semantic-only CF${=}$76).
18	% exp_ids: eval_70b_lora_scierc_s1_constrained, eval_70b_lora_docred_200_s1_constrained
19	LoRA confirms composability: DR\,$\to$\,0\% on both datasets (R-F1 44.53, 43.95; $|\Delta| \leq 0.20$\,pp); CF drops to 0.18--1.33\%, consistent with the 8B pattern (\S\ref{sec:composability}).
20	% exp_ids: eval_70b_lora_docred_200_beam4_s1
21	Beam ($k{=}4$) S1 at 70B yields dataset-dependent gains: on SciERC, R-F1 improves marginally ($+$0.28\,pp to 44.81); on DocRED, the boost is substantial ($+$4.19\,pp to 48.14) with all violations eliminated and parse failures dropping from 12.5\% to 0.5\% (Appendix~\ref{app:70b-ablation}).
22	
23	% exp_ids: trie_qwen_ft_scierc, trie_qwen_zs_scierc, trie_llama_ft_scierc, trie_llama_zs_scierc
24	\paragraph{Comparison with grammar-constrained approaches.}
25	\label{sec:constrained-baselines}
26	Our grammar-only ablation (Table~\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}, row~2) implements the same constraints as GCD~\cite{geng2023gcd} and BoostCD~\cite{boostcd2025}; vLLM \texttt{guided\_json}~\cite{kwon2023vllm} confirms equivalence (DR${=}$15.26\%, R-F1${=}$8.75; GCD-equiv.\ row, Appendix~\ref{app:gcd-comparison}).
27	The ${\sim}$15\% residual DR is the gap our semantic layer addresses (94\% total reduction).
28	Trie-only constraints without JSON enforcement yield 9--51\% parse failure (Appendix~\ref{app:trie-baseline}).
29	
```

    "originalFile": "% Runtime overhead (inline paragraph, no subsection header to save space)\n\n\\paragraph{Runtime overhead.}\n\\label{sec:runtime}\nS1 overhead is $+$13.1\\% on SciERC, lower on DocRED due to amortization over longer sequences; _source line 5725 · 2026-05-22T22:54:23.417Z_

[Director 指令] [Patch] Reviewer claim strength audit — 3 个低成本高回报的 claim 升级

**#1. "Preliminary 70B" 分层措辞（高影响）**

当前 Abstract/Intro/§5 统一用 "preliminary 70B validation"。但 SciERC 70B 已非 preliminary：551-doc 完整测试集、六路消融、order invariance 验证、FT+ZS 双 regime。

修改：
- 搜索所有 "preliminary" + "70B" 的组合
- SciERC 70B 相关改为 "comprehensive 70B validation"（或去掉 preliminary）
- DocRED 70B 保留 "preliminary"（200-doc subset）
- Abstract 可考虑："70B-class validation confirms scale-dependent composability on SciERC (full six-way ablation) with preliminary DocRED support"
- §5.4 WikiEvents 的 "(Preliminary)" 标题不改（那个确实是 preliminary）

**#2. Beam cross-scale claim（中高影响）**

70B beam4 S1 在两个数据集都 DR=0%。当前 beam 叙述只基于 8B。

在 §5 beam 段落或 appendix beam section 加一句：
"70B beam4 results confirm scale-independent violation elimination (DR=0% on both datasets at both 8B and 70B scales)."

**#3. Semantic layer 与模型能力正向互补（中影响）**

Semantic-only DR reduction: 8B ZS 24% → 70B ZS 84%（越强模型，semantic 层越有效）。

在 §7 Discussion 或 §5 70B 段加一句 observation：
"Semantic layer effectiveness scales with model capability: independent DR reduction increases from 24% (8B zero-shot) to 84% (70B zero-shot), suggesting that constraint enforcement benefits compound as base models improve."

**注意事项：**
- 主文 ≤ 8 页，这 3 个修改总共只加 2-3 句，应该没问题
- 如果空间紧张，#3 可以只放 appendix
- CF bound "≤1.4%" 保持不变（ROADMAP 的 ≤1.3% 是旧数据）
- 这次不用验证引用

编译确认后回复。

```text
1	% Runtime overhead (inline paragraph, no subsection header to save space)
2	
3	\paragraph{Runtime overhead.}
4	\label{sec:runtime}
5	S1 overhead is $+$13.1\% on SciERC, lower on DocRED due to amortization over longer sequences; under LoRA, $+$10.0\% with 37\% lower absolute latency.
6	Constraint compilation is one-time $<$0.5\,s; scaling benchmarks confirm constant overhead (2--3\%) up to 200 entity types and 1{,}000 relations (Appendix~\ref{app:scalability}).
7	
8	\paragraph{Beam search compatibility.}
9	\label{sec:beam-results}
10	With LoRA, beam search ($k{=}4$)~\cite{anderson2017guided} eliminates all four violation types on both SciERC and DocRED (R-F1 43--47; Appendix~\ref{app:beam-search}), yielding two deployment tiers: greedy S1 ($+$10\% latency, DR${<}$0.1\%, CF${\leq}$1.4\%) and beam S1 (1.54$\times$ latency, all violations${\to}$0\%; Table~\ref{tab:beam-latency}).
11	
12	% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_s1, r16_70b_docred_zs_s1, 70b_lora_scierc, 70b_lora_docred_200
13	\paragraph{Preliminary 70B-class validation.}
14	\label{sec:70b-results}
15	On Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct (AWQ INT4), zero-shot S1 eliminates DR on both datasets: SciERC 6.41\%\,$\to$\,0\% (R-F1 11.24), DocRED 14.98\%\,$\to$\,0\% (R-F1 13.05); CF rises to 0.04--3.50\%, comparable to 8B zero-shot CF.
16	% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_ablation (grammar_only, semantic_only, full_s1)
17	A four-way ablation on SciERC reveals scale-dependent layer interaction: semantic-only achieves 84\% DR reduction (6.41\%\,$\to$\,1.01\%), far exceeding the 8B result (24\%; Table~\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}), reflecting stronger internalized JSON generation; grammar remains necessary for RV elimination (0.61\%\,$\to$\,0\%) and CF control (semantic-only CF${=}$76).
18	% exp_ids: eval_70b_lora_scierc_s1_constrained, eval_70b_lora_docred_200_s1_constrained
19	LoRA confirms composability: DR\,$\to$\,0\% on both datasets (R-F1 44.53, 43.95; $|\Delta| \leq 0.20$\,pp); CF drops to 0.18--1.33\%, consistent with the 8B pattern (\S\ref{sec:composability}).
20	% exp_ids: eval_70b_lora_docred_200_beam4_s1
21	On DocRED, beam ($k{=}4$) S1 additionally reduces parse failures from 12.5\% to 0.5\%, contributing to a $+$4.19\,pp R-F1 improvement (43.95\,$\to$\,48.14); the controlled comparison on SciERC (0\% parse failure in both settings) isolates the pure beam quality gain at $+$0.28\,pp (Appendix~\ref{app:70b-ablation}).
22	
23	% exp_ids: trie_qwen_ft_scierc, trie_qwen_zs_scierc, trie_llama_ft_scierc, trie_llama_zs_scierc
24	\paragraph{Comparison with grammar-constrained approaches.}
25	\label{sec:constrained-baselines}
26	Our grammar-only ablation (Table~\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}, row~2) implements the same constraints as GCD~\cite{geng2023gcd} and BoostCD~\cite{boostcd2025}; vLLM \texttt{guided\_json}~\cite{kwon2023vllm} confirms equivalence (DR${=}$15.26\%, R-F1${=}$8.75; GCD-equiv.\ row, Appendix~\ref{app:gcd-comparison}).
27	The ${\sim}$15\% residual DR is the gap our semantic layer addresses (94\% total reduction).
28	Trie-only constraints without JSON enforcement yield 9--51\% parse failure (Appendix~\ref{app:trie-baseline}).
29	
```

### Tool result

_source line 5733 · 2026-05-22T22:54:35.055Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01F5mMGVcvJTa4VYa2k2EgH9`</summary>

```text
1	% Section 7 — Discussion and Conclusion
2	
3	OntoCoDec translates IE ontologies into composable token-level constraints, providing a systematic empirical framework---automatically compiled from ontologies, validated across 3 datasets, 3+ models, and 2 tasks---rather than a novel decoding algorithm.
4	LoRA + greedy S1 near-eliminates DR ($<$\,0.1\%) with CF\,$\leq$\,1.4\%; beam ($k{=}4$) eliminates all violations on both RE datasets (1.54$\times$ latency); zero-shot DR drops 42--99\% across three 8B-class models.
5	The central insight is that IE type constraints exceed context-free expressiveness (Proposition~1): unlike rejection-based approaches that prune invalid outputs post-generation~\cite{scholak2021picard}, the semantic layer applies constructive forward masking via a dynamic entity registry, bridging the gap grammar-only enforcement cannot address (3\% vs.\ 94\% DR reduction); this enabling dependency---scale-sensitive (\S\ref{sec:70b-results})---shows grammar-constrained decoding is necessary but insufficient for IE schema compliance.
6	Residual CF arises from per-triple independent enforcement; extending to global entity-level type consensus during decoding---eliminating CF without beam search overhead---is the most important direction for future work.
7

      "content": "% Section 7 — Discussion and Conclusion\n\nOntoCoDec translates IE ontologies into composable token-level constraints, providing a systematic empirical framework---automatically compiled from ontologies, validated across 3 datasets, 3+ models, and 2 tasks---rather than a novel decoding algorithm.\nLoRA + greedy S1 near-eliminates DR ($<$\\,0.1\\%) with CF\\,$\\leq$\\,1.4\\%; beam ($k{=}4$) eliminates all violations on both RE datasets (1.54$\\times$ latency); zero-shot DR drops 42--99\\% across three 8B-class models.\nThe central insight is that IE type constraints exceed context-free expressiveness (Proposition~1): unlike rejection-based approaches that prune invalid outputs post-generation~\\cite{scholak2021picard}, the semantic layer applies constructive forward masking via a dynamic entity registry, bridging the gap grammar-only enforcement cannot address (3\\% vs.\\ 94\\% DR reduction); this enabling dependency---scale-sensitive (\\S\\ref{sec:70b-results})---shows grammar-constrained decoding is necessary but insufficient for IE schema compliance.\nResidual CF arises from per-triple independent enforcement; extending to global entity-level type consensus during decoding---eliminating CF without beam search overhead---is the most important direction for future work.\n",
      "numLines": 7,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 7
    }
  },

    "newString": "\\paragraph{70B-class validation.}",
    "originalFile": "% Runtime overhead (inline paragraph, no subsection header to save space)\n\n\\paragraph{Runtime overhead.}\n\\label{sec:runtime}\nS1 overhead is $+$13.1\\% on SciERC, lower on DocRED due to amortization over longer sequences; under LoRA, $+$10.0\\% with 37\\% lower absolute latency.\nConstraint compilation is one-time $<$0.5\\,s; scaling benchmarks confirm constant overhead (2--3\\%) up to 200 entity types and 1{,}000 relations (Appendix~\\ref{app:scalability}).\n\n\\paragraph{Beam search compatibility.}\n\\label{sec:beam-results}\nWith LoRA, beam search ($k{=}4$)~\\cite{anderson2017guided} eliminates all four violation types on both SciERC and DocRED (R-F1 43--47; Appendix~\\ref{app:beam-search}), yielding two deployment tiers: greedy S1 ($+$10\\% latency, DR${<}$0.1\\%, CF${\\leq}$1.4\\%) and beam S1 (1.54$\\times$ latency, all violations${\\to}$0\\%; Table~\\ref{tab:beam-latency}).\n\n% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_s1, r16_70b_docred_zs_s1, 70b_lora_scierc, 70b_lora_docred_200\n\\paragraph{Preliminary 70B-class validation.}\n\\label{sec:70b-results}\nOn Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct (AWQ INT4), zero-shot S1 eliminates DR on both datasets: SciERC 6.41\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\% (R-F1 11.24), DocRED 14.98\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\% (R-F1 13.05); CF rises to 0.04--3.50\\%, comparable to 8B zero-shot CF.\n% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_ablation (grammar_only, semantic_only, full_s1)\nA four-way ablation on SciERC reveals scale-dependent layer interaction: semantic-only achieves 84\\% DR reduction (6.41\\%\\,$\\to$\\,1.01\\%), far exceeding the 8B result (24\\%; Table~\\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}), reflecting stronger internalized JSON generation; grammar remains necessary for RV elimination (0.61\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\%) and CF control (semantic-only CF${=}$76).\n% exp_ids: eval_70b_lora_scierc_s1_constrained, eval_70b_lora_docred_200_s1_constrained\nLoRA confirms composability: DR\\,$\\to$\\,0\\% on both datasets (R-F1 44.53, 43.95; $|\\Delta| \\leq 0.20$\\,pp); CF drops to 0.18--1.33\\%, consistent with the 8B pattern (\\S\\ref{sec:composability}).\n% exp_ids: eval_70b_lora_docred_200_beam4_s1\nOn DocRED, beam ($k{=}4$) S1 additionally reduces parse failures from 12.5\\% to 0.5\\%, contributing to a $+$4.19\\,pp R-F1 improvement (43.95\\,$\\to$\\,48.14); the controlled comparison on SciERC (0\\% parse failure in both settings) isolates the pure beam quality gain at $+$0.28\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:70b-ablation}).\n\n% exp_ids: trie_qwen_ft_scierc, trie_qwen_zs_scierc, trie_llama_ft_scierc, trie_llama_zs_scierc\n\\paragraph{Comparison with grammar-constrained approaches.}\n\\label{sec:constrained-baselines}\nOur grammar-only ablation (Table~\\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}, row~2) implements the same constraints as GCD~\\cite{geng2023gcd} and BoostCD~\\cite{boostcd2025}; vLLM \\texttt{guided\\_json}~\\cite{kwon2023vllm} confirms equivalence (DR${=}$15.26\\%, R-F1${=}$8.75; GCD-equiv.\\ row, Appendix~\\ref{app:gcd-comparison}).\nThe ${\\sim}$15\\% residual DR is the gap our semantic layer addresses (94\\% total reduction).\nTrie-only constraints without JSON enforcement yield 9--51\\% parse failure (Appendix~\\ref{app:trie-baseline}).\n",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01646pHNy1vqgT9m8UUv4nq5`</summary>

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	We present \textsc{OntoCoDec}, a framework that translates Information Extraction (IE) ontologies into composable token-level decoding constraints.
3	A decidability analysis yields a two-layer architecture: a \emph{grammar layer} (context-free) compiles ontology constraints into grammars ensuring JSON validity and vocabulary membership; a \emph{semantic layer} (prefix-decidable with state) interprets type constraints at runtime via stateful token masking over a dynamic entity registry, with grammar-first fallback when constraints conflict.
4	The two layers exhibit an empirical enabling dependency (Proposition~1, scale-sensitive; \S\ref{sec:70b-results}): neither achieves full DR reduction alone.
5	% exp_ids: w5_lora_scierc_constrained_eval, w5_qwen_docred_lora_constrained_eval, w5_llama_lora_scierc_constrained_eval, w5_llama_docred_lora_constrained_eval
6	Composed with LoRA fine-tuning on three 8B-class models across two RE datasets (up to 96 relation types), \textsc{OntoCoDec} near-eliminates DR ($<$\,0.1\%) at R-F1 41--47\% ($\pm$0.8\,pp); under fine-tuning, CF remains $\leq$\,1.4\% with greedy decoding and reaches zero with beam search ($k{=}4$) on both RE datasets (1.54$\times$ latency; \S\ref{sec:beam-results}).
7	Zero-shot evaluation serves as a diagnostic probe, revealing DR reduction of 76--99\% on SciERC and 42--90\% on DocRED across three models; constraint tightness ($\bar{\kappa}$) is a descriptive correlate of enforcement difficulty ($n{=}3$ schemas, $p{=}0.138$).
8	Preliminary 70B-class validation (\S\ref{sec:70b-results}) and event extraction on WikiEvents (\S\ref{sec:wikievents}) confirm generalizability beyond the primary 8B evaluation.
9	\end{abstract}
10	
```

_source line 5749 · 2026-05-22T22:54:51.545Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01EpSv2EJfzb5dhFAgithpSB`</summary>

```text
1	% Section 1: Introduction (compressed, 669→~550 words)
2	
3	\section{Introduction}
4	\label{sec:introduction}
5	
6	Large language models are increasingly used for information extraction~\cite{li2023codeie,sainz2024gollie,wei2023zeroshot}, but when generating relation triples $(h, r, t)$, neither entity necessarily carries a type consistent with the ontology's domain/range constraints for $r$.
7	We call such outputs \emph{domain/range violations}: structurally valid triples that are ontologically invalid.
8	Violations persist across the capability spectrum: 15.4\% of triples on SciERC~\cite{luan2018scierc} and 18.5\% on DocRED~\cite{yao2019docred} under zero-shot decoding, and 0.5--2\% even after LoRA fine-tuning; rates correlate with schema restrictiveness (few valid type pairs per relation) rather than ontology or model scale.
9	At competitive quality (R-F1 40--46\%), even 1--2\% residual violations compound across thousands of documents into systematic knowledge graph corruption, making reliable constraint enforcement necessary.
10	
11	Existing approaches leave a fundamental gap.
12	Schema-informed prompting reduces violations by 62\% on average but is per-relation unreliable (violations for \textsc{Evaluate-for} actually \emph{increase} by 5.8\,pp).
13	Grammar-constrained decoding~\cite{dong2025xgrammar,willard2023outlines,beurer-kellner2023lmql} ensures syntactic well-formedness but cannot encode cross-field semantic constraints.
14	Post-hoc filtering improves precision but discards up to 48\% of predictions including true positives (\S\ref{sec:cross-model}); at high rejection rates (e.g., Mistral DocRED 73.7\%), it becomes impractical.
15	Training-based approaches~\cite{sainz2024gollie,lu2022uie} internalize schema knowledge but provide only soft guidance without runtime enforcement; no method provides both semantic awareness and hard constraint enforcement.
16	
17	We propose \textbf{OntoCoDec}, a framework that translates IE ontologies into composable layers of decoding-time constraints.
18	A decidability analysis of IE type constraints (\S\ref{sec:decidability}) yields a two-layer architecture: the \emph{grammar layer} compiles ontology constraints into context-free grammars (JSON structure, vocabulary membership) via XGrammar; the \emph{semantic layer} interprets type constraints at runtime via dynamic logit masking over an entity registry, with an \emph{empirical enabling dependency} between layers.
19	OntoCoDec compiles constraints automatically from IE ontologies, requires no constraint-specific training, and is applicable to autoregressive LMs with logits access; we validate on three English datasets (up to 96 relation types), three 8B-class models across three tokenizer families, with preliminary 70B-class validation (\S\ref{sec:70b-results}).
20	LoRA + greedy S1 achieves near-complete DR elimination: DR\,$\leq$\,0.09\% at R-F1 41--47\% ($\pm$0.8\,pp, $+$10\% latency); beam ($k{=}4$) eliminates all violation types on both RE datasets under fine-tuning (1.54$\times$ latency).
21	DR enforcement converts ontologically invalid violations to individually valid but type-inconsistent ones (CF\,$\leq$\,1.4\% fine-tuned; \S\ref{sec:scierc-results}); zero-shot evaluation reveals DR drops 42--99\% across models and datasets, with 76--99\% on SciERC, but total violations (DR+CF) decrease by $\sim$31\%, reflecting conversion that fine-tuning resolves (\S\ref{sec:cross-model}).
22	
23	We make a systematic engineering contribution with theoretical grounding:
24	\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt]
25	    \item A two-layer constrained decoding framework: we formalize a well-known intuition that cross-field type constraints require runtime state beyond context-free expressiveness (Proposition~1), yielding pair-based sequential enforcement at each level (\S\ref{sec:decidability}). Constraints compile automatically from IE ontologies, validated on schemas of varying restrictiveness with up to 96 relation types.
26	    \item A comprehensive evaluation across 3 IE datasets, 3 models (8B-class), and 2 tasks, with preliminary 70B validation: under fine-tuning, DR\,$\leq$\,0.09\% at R-F1 41--47\% with CF\,$\leq$\,1.4\%; zero-shot ablation reveals an enabling dependency where on SciERC neither layer alone exceeds 24\% DR reduction (at 70B, semantic layer achieves 84\% independently; \S\ref{sec:70b-results}); event extraction on WikiEvents validates applicability beyond RE (\S\ref{sec:wikievents}).
27	\end{enumerate}
28	
```

    "newString": "70B-class validation on SciERC (full six-way ablation; \\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}) with preliminary DocRED support, and event extraction on WikiEvents (\\S\\ref{sec:wikievents}), confirm generalizability beyond the primary 8B evaluation.",
    "originalFile": "\\begin{abstract}\nWe present \\textsc{OntoCoDec}, a framework that translates Information Extraction (IE) ontologies into composable token-level decoding constraints.\nA decidability analysis yields a two-layer architecture: a \\emph{grammar layer} (context-free) compiles ontology constraints into grammars ensuring JSON validity and vocabulary membership; a \\emph{semantic layer} (prefix-decidable with state) interprets type constraints at runtime via stateful token masking over a dynamic entity registry, with grammar-first fallback when constraints conflict.\nThe two layers exhibit an empirical enabling dependency (Proposition~1, scale-sensitive; \\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}): neither achieves full DR reduction alone.\n% exp_ids: w5_lora_scierc_constrained_eval, w5_qwen_docred_lora_constrained_eval, w5_llama_lora_scierc_constrained_eval, w5_llama_docred_lora_constrained_eval\nComposed with LoRA fine-tuning on three 8B-class models across two RE datasets (up to 96 relation types), \\textsc{OntoCoDec} near-eliminates DR ($<$\\,0.1\\%) at R-F1 41--47\\% ($\\pm$0.8\\,pp); under fine-tuning, CF remains $\\leq$\\,1.4\\% with greedy decoding and reaches zero with beam search ($k{=}4$) on both RE datasets (1.54$\\times$ latency; \\S\\ref{sec:beam-results}).\nZero-shot evaluation serves as a diagnostic probe, revealing DR reduction of 76--99\\% on SciERC and 42--90\\% on DocRED across three models; constraint tightness ($\\bar{\\kappa}$) is a descriptive correlate of enforcement difficulty ($n{=}3$ schemas, $p{=}0.138$).\nPreliminary 70B-class validation (\\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}) and event extraction on WikiEvents (\\S\\ref{sec:wikievents}) confirm generalizability beyond the primary 8B evaluation.\n\\end{abstract}\n",

{
  "recorded_role": "user",
  "parentUuid": "408d5ee6-5690-4ffc-9cf0-fb64f9d39d1b",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "promptId": "7e276844-46e8-451c-9a0c-c1da160c36b7",
  "type": "user",
  "uuid": "5f4f83bd-3377-4fdf-9773-8d36a49cf016",
  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_6/projects/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/artifacts/_project/paper/section1_introduction_draft.tex",
    "oldString": "with preliminary 70B validation: under fine-tuning",
    "newString": "with 70B validation (full SciERC ablation, preliminary DocRED): under fine-tuning",
    "originalFile": "% Section 1: Introduction (compressed, 669→~550 words)\n\n\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nLarge language models are increasingly used for information extraction~\\cite{li2023codeie,sainz2024gollie,wei2023zeroshot}, but when generating relation triples $(h, r, t)$, neither entity necessarily carries a type consistent with the ontology's domain/range constraints for $r$.\nWe call such outputs \\emph{domain/range violations}: structurally valid triples that are ontologically invalid.\nViolations persist across the capability spectrum: 15.4\\% of triples on SciERC~\\cite{luan2018scierc} and 18.5\\% on DocRED~\\cite{yao2019docred} under zero-shot decoding, and 0.5--2\\% even after LoRA fine-tuning; rates correlate with schema restrictiveness (few valid type pairs per relation) rather than ontology or model scale.\nAt competitive quality (R-F1 40--46\\%), even 1--2\\% residual violations compound across thousands of documents into systematic knowledge graph corruption, making reliable constraint enforcement necessary.\n\nExisting approaches leave a fundamental gap.\nSchema-informed prompting reduces violations by 62\\% on average but is per-relation unreliable (violations for \\textsc{Evaluate-for} actually \\emph{increase} by 5.8\\,pp).\nGrammar-constrained decoding~\\cite{dong2025xgrammar,willard2023outlines,beurer-kellner2023lmql} ensures syntactic well-formedness but cannot encode cross-field semantic constraints.\nPost-hoc filtering improves precision but discards up to 48\\% of predictions including true positives (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-model}); at high rejection rates (e.g., Mistral DocRED 73.7\\%), it becomes impractical.\nTraining-based approaches~\\cite{sainz2024gollie,lu2022uie} internalize schema knowledge but provide only soft guidance without runtime enforcement; no method provides both semantic awareness and hard constraint enforcement.\n\nWe propose \\textbf{OntoCoDec}, a framework that translates IE ontologies into composable layers of decoding-time constraints.\nA decidability analysis of IE type constraints (\\S\\ref{sec:decidability}) yields a two-layer architecture: the \\emph{grammar layer} compiles ontology constraints into context-free grammars (JSON structure, vocabulary membership) via XGrammar; the \\emph{semantic layer} interprets type constraints at runtime via dynamic logit masking over an entity registry, with an \\emph{empirical enabling dependency} between layers.\nOntoCoDec compiles constraints automatically from IE ontologies, requires no constraint-specific training, and is applicable to autoregressive LMs with logits access; we validate on three English datasets (up to 96 relation types), three 8B-class models across three tokenizer families, with 70B-class validation (\\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}).\nLoRA + greedy S1 achieves near-complete DR elimination: DR\\,$\\leq$\\,0.09\\% at R-F1 41--47\\% ($\\pm$0.8\\,pp, $+$10\\% latency); beam ($k{=}4$) eliminates all violation types on both RE datasets under fine-tuning (1.54$\\times$ latency).\nDR enforcement converts ontologically invalid violations to individually valid but type-inconsistent ones (CF\\,$\\leq$\\,1.4\\% fine-tuned; \\S\\ref{sec:scierc-results}); zero-shot evaluation reveals DR drops 42--99\\% across models and datasets, with 76--99\\% on SciERC, but total violations (DR+CF) decrease by $\\sim$31\\%, reflecting conversion that fine-tuning resolves (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-model}).\n\nWe make a systematic engineering contribution with theoretical grounding:\n\\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt]\n    \\item A two-layer constrained decoding framework: we formalize a well-known intuition that cross-field type constraints require runtime state beyond context-free expressiveness (Proposition~1), yielding pair-based sequential enforcement at each level (\\S\\ref{sec:decidability}). Constraints compile automatically from IE ontologies, validated on schemas of varying restrictiveness with up to 96 relation types.\n    \\item A comprehensive evaluation across 3 IE datasets, 3 models (8B-class), and 2 tasks, with preliminary 70B validation: under fine-tuning, DR\\,$\\leq$\\,0.09\\% at R-F1 41--47\\% with CF\\,$\\leq$\\,1.4\\%; zero-shot ablation reveals an enabling dependency where on SciERC neither layer alone exceeds 24\\% DR reduction (at 70B, semantic layer achieves 84\\% independently; \\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}); event extraction on WikiEvents validates applicability beyond RE (\\S\\ref{sec:wikievents}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",
    "structuredPatch": [
      {

        "oldLines": 5,
        "newStart": 23,

          "     \\item A two-layer constrained decoding framework: we formalize a well-known intuition that cross-field type constraints require runtime state beyond context-free expressiveness (Proposition~1), yielding pair-based sequential enforcement at each level (\\S\\ref{sec:decidability}). Constraints compile automatically from IE ontologies, validated on schemas of varying restrictiveness with up to 96 relation types.",
          "-    \\item A comprehensive evaluation across 3 IE datasets, 3 models (8B-class), and 2 tasks, with preliminary 70B validation: under fine-tuning, DR\\,$\\leq$\\,0.09\\% at R-F1 41--47\\% with CF\\,$\\leq$\\,1.4\\%; zero-shot ablation reveals an enabling dependency where on SciERC neither layer alone exceeds 24\\% DR reduction (at 70B, semantic layer achieves 84\\% independently; \\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}); event extraction on WikiEvents validates applicability beyond RE (\\S\\ref{sec:wikievents}).",
          "+    \\item A comprehensive evaluation across 3 IE datasets, 3 models (8B-class), and 2 tasks, with 70B validation (full SciERC ablation, preliminary DocRED): under fine-tuning, DR\\,$\\leq$\\,0.09\\% at R-F1 41--47\\% with CF\\,$\\leq$\\,1.4\\%; zero-shot ablation reveals an enabling dependency where on SciERC neither layer alone exceeds 24\\% DR reduction (at 70B, semantic layer achieves 84\\% independently; \\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}); event extraction on WikiEvents validates applicability beyond RE (\\S\\ref{sec:wikievents}).",

1	% Runtime overhead (inline paragraph, no subsection header to save space)
2	
3	\paragraph{Runtime overhead.}
4	\label{sec:runtime}
5	S1 overhead is $+$13.1\% on SciERC, lower on DocRED due to amortization over longer sequences; under LoRA, $+$10.0\% with 37\% lower absolute latency.
6	Constraint compilation is one-time $<$0.5\,s; scaling benchmarks confirm constant overhead (2--3\%) up to 200 entity types and 1{,}000 relations (Appendix~\ref{app:scalability}).
7	
8	\paragraph{Beam search compatibility.}
9	\label{sec:beam-results}
10	With LoRA, beam search ($k{=}4$)~\cite{anderson2017guided} eliminates all four violation types on both SciERC and DocRED (R-F1 43--47; Appendix~\ref{app:beam-search}), yielding two deployment tiers: greedy S1 ($+$10\% latency, DR${<}$0.1\%, CF${\leq}$1.4\%) and beam S1 (1.54$\times$ latency, all violations${\to}$0\%; Table~\ref{tab:beam-latency}).
11	70B beam4 confirms scale-independent violation elimination (DR${=}$0\% on both datasets at both 8B and 70B; \S\ref{sec:70b-results}).
12	
13	% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_s1, r16_70b_docred_zs_s1, 70b_lora_scierc, 70b_lora_docred_200
14	\paragraph{70B-class validation.}
15	\label{sec:70b-results}
16	On Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct (AWQ INT4), zero-shot S1 eliminates DR on both datasets: SciERC 6.41\%\,$\to$\,0\% (R-F1 11.24), DocRED 14.98\%\,$\to$\,0\% (R-F1 13.05); CF rises to 0.04--3.50\%, comparable to 8B zero-shot CF.
17	% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_ablation (grammar_only, semantic_only, full_s1)
18	A four-way ablation on SciERC reveals scale-dependent layer interaction: semantic-only achieves 84\% DR reduction (6.41\%\,$\to$\,1.01\%), far exceeding the 8B result (24\%; Table~\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}), reflecting stronger internalized JSON generation; grammar remains necessary for RV elimination (0.61\%\,$\to$\,0\%) and CF control (semantic-only CF${=}$76).
19	% exp_ids: eval_70b_lora_scierc_s1_constrained, eval_70b_lora_docred_200_s1_constrained
20	LoRA confirms composability: DR\,$\to$\,0\% on both datasets (R-F1 44.53, 43.95; $|\Delta| \leq 0.20$\,pp); CF drops to 0.18--1.33\%, consistent with the 8B pattern (\S\ref{sec:composability}).
21	% exp_ids: eval_70b_lora_docred_200_beam4_s1
22	On DocRED, beam ($k{=}4$) S1 additionally reduces parse failures from 12.5\% to 0.5\%, contributing to a $+$4.19\,pp R-F1 improvement (43.95\,$\to$\,48.14); the controlled comparison on SciERC (0\% parse failure in both settings) isolates the pure beam quality gain at $+$0.28\,pp (Appendix~\ref{app:70b-ablation}).
23	
24	% exp_ids: trie_qwen_ft_scierc, trie_qwen_zs_scierc, trie_llama_ft_scierc, trie_llama_zs_scierc
25	\paragraph{Comparison with grammar-constrained approaches.}
26	\label{sec:constrained-baselines}
27	Our grammar-only ablation (Table~\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}, row~2) implements the same constraints as GCD~\cite{geng2023gcd} and BoostCD~\cite{boostcd2025}; vLLM \texttt{guided\_json}~\cite{kwon2023vllm} confirms equivalence (DR${=}$15.26\%, R-F1${=}$8.75; GCD-equiv.\ row, Appendix~\ref{app:gcd-comparison}).
28	The ${\sim}$15\% residual DR is the gap our semantic layer addresses (94\% total reduction).
29	Trie-only constraints without JSON enforcement yield 9--51\% parse failure (Appendix~\ref{app:trie-baseline}).
30

      "content": "% Runtime overhead (inline paragraph, no subsection header to save space)\n\n\\paragraph{Runtime overhead.}\n\\label{sec:runtime}\nS1 overhead is $+$13.1\\% on SciERC, lower on DocRED due to amortization over longer sequences; under LoRA, $+$10.0\\% with 37\\% lower absolute latency.\nConstraint compilation is one-time $<$0.5\\,s; scaling benchmarks confirm constant overhead (2--3\\%) up to 200 entity types and 1{,}000 relations (Appendix~\\ref{app:scalability}).\n\n\\paragraph{Beam search compatibility.}\n\\label{sec:beam-results}\nWith LoRA, beam search ($k{=}4$)~\\cite{anderson2017guided} eliminates all four violation types on both SciERC and DocRED (R-F1 43--47; Appendix~\\ref{app:beam-search}), yielding two deployment tiers: greedy S1 ($+$10\\% latency, DR${<}$0.1\\%, CF${\\leq}$1.4\\%) and beam S1 (1.54$\\times$ latency, all violations${\\to}$0\\%; Table~\\ref{tab:beam-latency}).\n70B beam4 confirms scale-independent violation elimination (DR${=}$0\\% on both datasets at both 8B and 70B; \\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}).\n\n% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_s1, r16_70b_docred_zs_s1, 70b_lora_scierc, 70b_lora_docred_200\n\\paragraph{70B-class validation.}\n\\label{sec:70b-results}\nOn Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct (AWQ INT4), zero-shot S1 eliminates DR on both datasets: SciERC 6.41\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\% (R-F1 11.24), DocRED 14.98\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\% (R-F1 13.05); CF rises to 0.04--3.50\\%, comparable to 8B zero-shot CF.\n% exp_ids: r16_70b_scierc_zs_ablation (grammar_only, semantic_only, full_s1)\nA four-way ablation on SciERC reveals scale-dependent layer interaction: semantic-only achieves 84\\% DR reduction (6.41\\%\\,$\\to$\\,1.01\\%), far exceeding the 8B result (24\\%; Table~\\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}), reflecting stronger internalized JSON generation; grammar remains necessary for RV elimination (0.61\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\%) and CF control (semantic-only CF${=}$76).\n% exp_ids: eval_70b_lora_scierc_s1_constrained, eval_70b_lora_docred_200_s1_constrained\nLoRA confirms composability: DR\\,$\\to$\\,0\\% on both datasets (R-F1 44.53, 43.95; $|\\Delta| \\leq 0.20$\\,pp); CF drops to 0.18--1.33\\%, consistent with the 8B pattern (\\S\\ref{sec:composability}).\n% exp_ids: eval_70b_lora_docred_200_beam4_s1\nOn DocRED, beam ($k{=}4$) S1 additionally reduces parse failures from 12.5\\% to 0.5\\%, contributing to a $+$4.19\\,pp R-F1 improvement (43.95\\,$\\to$\\,48.14); the controlled comparison on SciERC (0\\% parse failure in both settings) isolates the pure beam quality gain at $+$0.28\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:70b-ablation}).\n\n% exp_ids: trie_qwen_ft_scierc, trie_qwen_zs_scierc, trie_llama_ft_scierc, trie_llama_zs_scierc\n\\paragraph{Comparison with grammar-constrained approaches.}\n\\label{sec:constrained-baselines}\nOur grammar-only ablation (Table~\\ref{tab:scierc-ablation}, row~2) implements the same constraints as GCD~\\cite{geng2023gcd} and BoostCD~\\cite{boostcd2025}; vLLM \\texttt{guided\\_json}~\\cite{kwon2023vllm} confirms equivalence (DR${=}$15.26\\%, R-F1${=}$8.75; GCD-equiv.\\ row, Appendix~\\ref{app:gcd-comparison}).\nThe ${\\sim}$15\\% residual DR is the gap our semantic layer addresses (94\\% total reduction).\nTrie-only constraints without JSON enforcement yield 9--51\\% parse failure (Appendix~\\ref{app:trie-baseline}).\n",

13	\small
14	\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3.6pt}
15	\begin{tabular}{@{}l rrrr rr@{}}

22	Grammar       & 1.50 & 0 & 0 & 0    & 44.63 & 64.82 \\
23	Semantic      & 0.60 & 0 & 0 & 9    & 44.73 & 64.86 \\
24	S1            & \textbf{0} & 0 & 0 & 15 & 44.53 & 64.76 \\
25	S2            & \textbf{0} & 0 & 0 & 18 & 36.28 & 62.35 \\
26	Beam4 S1      & \textbf{0} & 0 & 0 & \textbf{1} & \textbf{44.81} & \textbf{64.89} \\

29	\caption{70B-class (Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct (AWQ INT4)) LoRA ablation on SciERC (551 docs).
30	CF: violation count. F1 $\times$100.}
31	\label{tab:70b-ft-ablation}
32	\end{table}
33	
34	The grammar layer provides zero DR reduction (1.50\%\,$\to$\,1.50\%), consistent with the zero-shot 70B pattern where fine-tuned models already produce structurally valid JSON (\S\ref{sec:70b-results}).
35	The semantic layer is the sole effective defense (1.50\%\,$\to$\,0.60\%, 60\% reduction) with no R-F1 cost ($\Delta{=}$0\,pp), and full S1 eliminates all DR.
36	S2 eliminates DR but causes severe R-F1 degradation ($-$8.45\,pp), indicating that optimal constraint granularity is scale-dependent: the additional S2 constraints become overly restrictive at 70B, where stronger baseline generation makes fine-grained intervention counterproductive.
37	Beam ($k{=}4$) S1 remains the optimal configuration (DR${=}$0\%, CF${=}$1, R-F1${=}$44.81).
38	Notably, S2 increases CF from 15 (S1) to 18 despite its cross-field consistency design, suggesting that over-constraining induces cascading generation degradation that creates new violations.
39

67	
68	The $+$4.19\,pp R-F1 gain from beam search on DocRED (43.95\,$\to$\,48.14) is largely attributable to parse failure recovery: greedy S1 fails to parse 12.5\% of documents (25/200), while beam reduces this to 0.5\% (1/200), recovering $\sim$24 documents that contribute no triples under greedy decoding.
69	The controlled comparison on SciERC---where both greedy and beam achieve 0\% parse failure---isolates the pure beam quality gain at $+$0.28\,pp, consistent with the 8B pattern (Appendix~\ref{app:beam-search}).

5	\label{sec:violation_landscape}
6	
7	We evaluate OntoCoDec in two deployment configurations: LoRA + greedy S1 (\S\ref{sec:composability}), near-eliminating DR (DR\,$\leq$\,0.09\%) with minimal overhead; and LoRA + beam S1 (\S\ref{sec:beam-results}), achieving zero total violations on SciERC (1.54$\times$ latency).
8	Zero-shot evaluation (\S\ref{sec:scierc-results}--\S\ref{sec:cross-model}) serves as a diagnostic probe; the deployment-relevant evaluation is the fine-tuned setting (R-F1 40--46\%).
9	
10	\paragraph{Setup.}
11	We evaluate on SciERC~\cite{luan2018scierc} (551 test sentences, 7 relations, $\bar{\kappa}{=}0.333$) and DocRED~\cite{yao2019docred} (998 dev documents,\footnote{DocRED test annotations are not publicly available; we evaluate on the development set.} 96 relations, $\bar{\kappa}{=}0.842$).
12	RE type constraints are extracted automatically from observed type pairs in training data (neither dataset provides a formal ontology); EE constraints on WikiEvents (\S\ref{sec:wikievents}) are derived from the KAIROS ontology.
13	All constraints are defined from schema-level supervision, independently of the evaluation split.
14	The framework handles both sources without modification.
15	Primary model: Qwen3-8B~\cite{qwen2025qwen3}, zero-shot greedy decoding; cross-model validation in \S\ref{sec:cross-model}, composability with fine-tuning in \S\ref{sec:composability}.
16	We measure four violation categories: \textbf{DR} (domain/range type pair $\notin \sigma(r)$), \textbf{RV} (relation $\notin \mathcal{R}$), \textbf{ET} (entity type $\notin \mathcal{E}$), and \textbf{CF} (same entity assigned conflicting types across triples).
17	Baseline DR: 15.37\% (SciERC), 18.46\% (DocRED); schema-informed prompting reduces DR by 62\% on SciERC but is per-relation unreliable (Appendix~\ref{app:per-relation-landscape}).
18

    "originalFile": "% Section 1: Introduction (compressed, 669→~550 words)\n\n\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nLarge language models are increasingly used for information extraction~\\cite{li2023codeie,sainz2024gollie,wei2023zeroshot}, but when generating relation triples $(h, r, t)$, neither entity necessarily carries a type consistent with the ontology's domain/range constraints for $r$.\nWe call such outputs \\emph{domain/range violations}: structurally valid triples that are ontologically invalid.\nViolations persist across the capability spectrum: 15.4\\% of triples on SciERC~\\cite{luan2018scierc} and 18.5\\% on DocRED~\\cite{yao2019docred} under zero-shot decoding, and 0.5--2\\% even after LoRA fine-tuning; rates correlate with schema restrictiveness (few valid type pairs per relation) rather than ontology or model scale.\nAt competitive quality (R-F1 40--46\\%), even 1--2\\% residual violations compound across thousands of documents into systematic knowledge graph corruption, making reliable constraint enforcement necessary.\n\nExisting approaches leave a fundamental gap.\nSchema-informed prompting reduces violations by 62\\% on average but is per-relation unreliable (violations for \\textsc{Evaluate-for} actually \\emph{increase} by 5.8\\,pp).\nGrammar-constrained decoding~\\cite{dong2025xgrammar,willard2023outlines,beurer-kellner2023lmql} ensures syntactic well-formedness but cannot encode cross-field semantic constraints.\nPost-hoc filtering improves precision but discards up to 48\\% of predictions including true positives (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-model}); at high rejection rates (e.g., Mistral DocRED 73.7\\%), it becomes impractical.\nTraining-based approaches~\\cite{sainz2024gollie,lu2022uie} internalize schema knowledge but provide only soft guidance without runtime enforcement; no method provides both semantic awareness and hard constraint enforcement.\n\nWe propose \\textbf{OntoCoDec}, a framework that translates IE ontologies into composable layers of decoding-time constraints.\nA decidability analysis of IE type constraints (\\S\\ref{sec:decidability}) yields a two-layer architecture: the \\emph{grammar layer} compiles ontology constraints into context-free grammars (JSON structure, vocabulary membership) via XGrammar; the \\emph{semantic layer} interprets type constraints at runtime via dynamic logit masking over an entity registry, with an \\emph{empirical enabling dependency} between layers.\nOntoCoDec compiles constraints automatically from IE ontologies, requires no constraint-specific training, and is applicable to autoregressive LMs with logits access; we validate on three English datasets (up to 96 relation types), three 8B-class models across three tokenizer families, with 70B-class validation (\\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}).\nLoRA + greedy S1 achieves near-complete DR elimination: DR\\,$\\leq$\\,0.09\\% at R-F1 41--47\\% ($\\pm$0.8\\,pp, $+$10\\% latency); beam ($k{=}4$) eliminates all violation types on both RE datasets under fine-tuning (1.54$\\times$ latency).\nDR enforcement converts ontologically invalid violations to individually valid but type-inconsistent ones (CF\\,$\\leq$\\,1.4\\% fine-tuned; \\S\\ref{sec:scierc-results}); zero-shot evaluation reveals DR drops 42--99\\% across models and datasets, with 76--99\\% on SciERC, but total violations (DR+CF) decrease by $\\sim$31\\%, reflecting conversion that fine-tuning resolves (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-model}).\n\nWe make a systematic engineering contribution with theoretical grounding:\n\\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt]\n    \\item A two-layer constrained decoding framework: we formalize a well-known intuition that cross-field type constraints require runtime state beyond context-free expressiveness (Proposition~1), yielding pair-based sequential enforcement at each level (\\S\\ref{sec:decidability}). Constraints compile automatically from IE ontologies, validated on schemas of varying restrictiveness with up to 96 relation types.\n    \\item A comprehensive evaluation across 3 IE datasets, 3 models (8B-class), and 2 tasks, with 70B validation (full SciERC ablation, preliminary DocRED): under fine-tuning, DR\\,$\\leq$\\,0.09\\% at R-F1 41--47\\% with CF\\,$\\leq$\\,1.4\\%; zero-shot ablation reveals an enabling dependency where on SciERC neither layer alone exceeds 24\\% DR reduction (at 70B, semantic layer achieves 84\\% independently; \\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}); event extraction on WikiEvents validates applicability beyond RE (\\S\\ref{sec:wikievents}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",

  "content": "[Director 指令] [Patch] 不等实验数据——先修主文 grammar layer 措辞矛盾\n\n**问题：** 主文 §5 写 grammar layer \"provides zero DR reduction at 70B\"（基于 SciERC DR 1.5%→1.5%）。但 DocRED ZS 数据显示 grammar-only DR 从 13.45% *增加*到 17.03%（+3.58pp）。审稿人会抓这个矛盾。\n\n**修复 §5.5 或 §4 相关段落：**\n当前：\"grammar layer provides zero DR contribution\"（或类似措辞）\n改为：\"grammar layer provides zero-to-negative independent DR contribution at 70B (unchanged on SciERC; +3.58pp on DocRED zero-shot due to RV$\\to$DR conversion in high-$\\bar{\\kappa}$ schemas)\"\n\n如果空间不够放完整解释，可以简化为：\n\"grammar layer provides no independent DR benefit at 70B (§\\ref{app:70b-ablation})\"\n\n**appendix_70b_ablation.tex — ZS DocRED 数据写入：**\n在 DocRED section（tab:70b-docred-ablation 附近或新建 ZS panel），加入以下数据：\n\n| Config | DR% | RV% | ET% | CF | R-F1 | E-F1 |\n|--------|-----|-----|-----|-----|------|------|\n| ZS Unconstrained | 13.45 | 7.88 | 0 | 0 | 13.25 | 70.14 |\n| ZS Grammar-only | 17.03 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 13.23 | 70.12 |\n| ZS Semantic-only | 1.66 | 8.24 | 132 | — | 13.05 | 70.10 |\n\n分析段落（2-3 句）：\n- Grammar-only 消除 RV (7.88%→0%) 但 DR 反升 3.58pp：forced vocabulary mapping 在高 κ̄ schema (DocRED κ̄=0.842, ~16% type pairs valid) 下将 RV 转化为 DR\n- Semantic-only 独立实现 87.7% DR reduction（13.45%→1.66%），R-F1 无损\n- 两层联合（full S1）的必要性由此进一步确认——grammar 为 semantic 提供 RV 消除基础，semantic 消除 DR，缺一不可\n\n编译确认后回复。这次不用验证引用。"
}
